🎯 What We LearnIntroduction

What We Learn in Chapter 1: किरन

1. What you will learn

In this lesson, you will learn about:

  • How to understand and interpret stories about किरन.
  • The importance of different times of the day like morning, evening, and night.
  • How to identify the activities किरन does at different times.
  • The significance of the environment and nature, such as trees and animals, in her life.
  • How to express thoughts and observations about किरन's routine and surroundings.

2. Simple explanation

This chapter is about किरन and her daily life. It describes how she wakes up early, plays, studies, and goes to sleep. It also talks about the special things around her, like trees and animals, and how these things help her. We learn how to read stories carefully and understand what they tell us about her day and environment.

3. Step-by-step breakdown

  • Understanding किरन’s routine: How she wakes up, plays, studies, and sleeps.
  • Time of day: Recognizing morning, evening, and night in her story.
  • Activities at different times: What किरन does in the morning, evening, and night.
  • Environment and nature: How trees and animals are part of her life.
  • Expressing observations: How to describe what we see and hear in stories.

4. Worked example

Question:

The story tells us that किरन wakes up early in the morning and plays with her friends. She then goes to school, studies, and comes back home in the evening. At night, she sleeps peacefully. Draw a sequence chart showing the activities of किरन during the day.

Solution:

  • Step 1: Identify the main activities: waking up, playing, going to school, studying, coming home, sleeping.
  • Step 2: Arrange these activities in order:
  • Wake up early (morning).
  • Play with friends.
  • Go to school.
  • Study at school.
  • Return home in the evening.
  • Sleep at night.
  • Step 3: Draw a flowchart or sequence diagram with these activities in order, showing the progression of her day.

Conceptual interpretation:

This sequence helps us understand how a typical day is organized for किरन and how different times of the day influence her activities.

5. Common mistake

Many students think that all activities happen at the same time. Remember, activities are done at different times of the day, like morning or evening. It is important to understand the sequence and timing to correctly interpret stories.

6. Quick check question

Can you tell me what activities you do in the morning and at night? How are they different?

7. Summary

In this lesson, we learned about किरन’s daily routine and the importance of recognizing different times of the day. We understood how her activities change from morning to night and how nature plays a role in her life. Practice observing your own daily routine and describe it in order. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

🧪 Worked ExamplesExamples

What You Will Learn

Today, we will learn how to understand and interpret the examples from the story of Kiran, focusing on how to read and analyze the details in the story, such as activities, times, and emotions. We will also learn how to answer questions based on these examples.

Simple Explanation

In the story of Kiran, many small details tell us about her day, her feelings, and her activities. By carefully reading the story, we can find answers to questions about what she did, when she did it, and how she felt. These details help us understand the story better and answer questions related to it.

Step-by-step Breakdown

1. Read the story carefully: Focus on what Kiran is doing and what others are doing around her.

2. Identify key details: Look for words that tell us about time (morning, evening, night), activities (playing, sleeping, waking up), and feelings (happy, tired).

3. Answer questions based on details: Use the clues from the story to answer questions about her activities, feelings, and the time of day.

4. Look for clues in the story: For example, if Kiran is sleeping, it is likely night or early morning.

5. Connect details logically: Think about how activities follow each other and what they tell us about her day.

Worked Example

Question:

The story says, "Kiran woke up early and went to school. She played with her friends in the evening and then went home." Based on this, answer the following:

  • What time of day did Kiran wake up?
  • What activity did she do in the evening?
  • How do we know she went home after playing?

Step 1:

Kiran woke up early, so it was morning.

Step 2:

In the evening, she played with her friends, which shows her activity during that time.

Step 3:

The story says she went home after playing, so we connect the activity of playing with going home.

Step 4:

From these clues, we understand her day started in the morning and ended at night when she went home.

Conceptual interpretation:

This shows how stories tell us about different times of the day through activities and words. Recognizing these clues helps us understand the story better and answer questions accurately.

Common Mistake

Many students confuse activities that happen at night with those in the morning. Remember, sleeping or waking up clues are important. For example, if someone is sleeping, it is likely night or early morning, not afternoon.

Quick Check Question

Kiran said, "I am happy because I played in the park in the afternoon."

  • What activity did she do?
  • How do you know it was afternoon?

Summary

In this lesson, we learned how to read stories carefully and find clues about activities and times of day. This helps us answer questions confidently and understand stories better. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

📌 RecapSummary

What you will learn

Today, we will review the main ideas from the chapter "किरन" to help you understand what you have learned so far. This will include understanding how किरन's day is described, the activities she does, and the important details from the story.

Simple explanation

In this recap, we will go over the story of किरन, her daily routine, and the questions that help us understand her day better. We will also look at how to answer questions about her activities, time, and feelings based on the story.

Step-by-step breakdown

1. Understanding the story: किरन wakes up early, plays, studies, and goes to sleep. Her day is full of activities, and the story describes her feelings and actions.

2. Important details: The story mentions specific times like morning and evening, and activities like playing, studying, and sleeping.

3. Questions and answers: The questions help us think about how किरन feels, what she does, and when she does these activities.

4. Visual clues: The story uses words like "सुबह" (morning) and "शाम" (evening) to tell us about different times of the day.

5. Activities: We learn about how किरन helps her family, plays with friends, and studies.

6. Understanding feelings: The story shows किरन is happy when she plays and sleepy when she sleeps.

Worked example

Question:

The story mentions that किरन wakes up early and plays with her sister in the evening. Based on this, answer the following:

  • When does किरन wake up?
  • What activities does she do in the evening?
  • How does she feel when she sleeps?

Solution:

  • Step 1: The story says किरन wakes up early in the morning.
  • Step 2: In the evening, she plays with her sister and helps her family.
  • Step 3: When she sleeps, she feels sleepy but happy.
  • Explanation: The story describes her waking up early, playing in the evening, and feeling sleepy when she sleeps. This helps us understand her daily routine and feelings.

Common mistake

Many students might think कि किरन only plays or only studies. Remember, she does many activities throughout the day, like waking up, playing, studying, helping, and sleeping. Always look for clues in the story to understand her full day.

Quick check question

What activities does किरन do in the story, and at what times of the day? Think about her morning, afternoon, and evening activities.

Summary

Today, we revisited the story of किरन, focusing on her daily routine, activities, and feelings. Remember to read carefully and look for clues about time and actions. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

Q: किरन को कहानी में कौन सी समस्या का सामना करना पड़ा?
  • स्कूल से घर लौटते समय किरन को रास्ता खोने की समस्या हुई।
  • गाँव के बीच एक छोटी दरार थी जहाँ उसे रुकना पड़ा।
  • वह दिन के अंत में अपने पापा के काम में सहायता करने के लिए देर हो गया था।
  • किरन को लगता था कि वह अपनी ज़िम्मेदारियाँ पूरी नहीं कर सकता।

Q: What is the difference between 'सुबह' and 'शाम'?

📖 10. तीन मछलियाँ
✅ RAG (9 chunks)LKB: ✅DKB: 25 entries
✅RAG source material found — 9 chunks indexed from textbook PDF.

🎯 What We LearnIntroduction

What We Learn

In this lesson, we will understand how different types of fish think and behave in a pond or lake. We will learn about the different ways fish react to danger, how they plan to stay safe, and how they behave when they see a threat. We will also explore how fish can plan and make decisions, just like humans do, to protect themselves from predators.

---

Simple Explanation

In the story of "Three Fish," each fish has a different way of thinking about danger. One fish is very clever and plans ahead to stay safe. Another fish is quick and reacts instantly to danger. The third fish believes that whatever will happen, will happen, and does not worry much about danger. The story shows us how each fish tries to stay safe in its own way, and how they plan or react when they see a threat.

---

Step-by-step Breakdown

1. Introduction to the Fish Characters:

  • There are three fish in a beautiful pond.
  • Each fish has a different way of thinking about danger.

2. The First Fish: The Clever Fish (Anagat Ladhata):

  • This fish is very wise.
  • It thinks about problems before they happen.
  • It plans how to stay safe from predators in advance.
  • It is like a wise person who prepares for trouble before it comes.

3. The Second Fish: The Quick-reacting Fish (Pratyutapan Mlat):

  • This fish is very sharp and fast.
  • It reacts immediately when it sees danger.
  • It doesn’t think much; it just acts quickly.
  • It is like a person who responds instantly to a sudden problem.

4. The Third Fish: The Optimistic Fish (Yadbhallvashaya):

  • This fish believes that whatever will happen, will happen.
  • It does not worry about danger.
  • It thinks, “Whatever happens, will happen,” and stays calm.
  • It is like someone who trusts that everything will be fine.

5. The Predators Arrive:

  • One day, some fishermen come with nets to catch fish.
  • The fish get scared when they see the fishermen.

6. How Each Fish Reacts:

  • The clever fish plans to escape early.
  • The quick fish reacts instantly and tries to hide.
  • The optimistic fish stays calm and believes it will be safe.

7. Outcome of Their Actions:

  • The clever fish manages to escape because it planned ahead.
  • The quick fish also escapes because it reacted fast.
  • The optimistic fish stays calm but gets caught because it didn’t prepare or react.

8. Lesson from the Story:

  • Planning ahead and reacting quickly can help us stay safe.
  • Believing everything will be fine is good, but it is also important to be prepared.

---

Worked Example

Suppose the fishermen come at 6 a.m. to catch fish.

  • The clever fish thinks, “They are coming, I should hide now,” and hides early.
  • The quick-reacting fish sees the net and immediately swims away.
  • The optimistic fish thinks, “Nothing will happen,” and stays in the same place, so it gets caught.

Question: Which fish is most likely to escape?

Answer: The clever fish and the quick-reacting fish, because they are prepared or respond fast. The optimistic fish might stay safe if it reacts or plans, but in this case, it did not.

---

Common Mistake

Many students think that the optimistic fish is always safe because it believes everything will be fine. But in reality, just believing without planning or reacting quickly can be dangerous. It’s important to balance faith with preparation and quick response.

---

Quick Check Question

Imagine you are a fish in a pond. A predator comes near.

  • What should you do if you want to stay safe?

(a) Stay calm and do nothing.

(b) Plan ahead or react quickly.

(c) Believe everything will be fine.

Choose the best answer and explain why.

---

Summary

Today, we learned about three different types of fish and how they react to danger. The clever fish plans ahead, the quick fish reacts instantly, and the optimistic fish believes everything will be fine. Understanding these behaviors he

🧪 Worked ExamplesExamples

Lesson on Worked Examples from Chapter 10: तीन मछलियाँ

What you will learn

  • How to analyze and solve questions based on the story of three fishes.
  • How to understand the reasoning behind plans and actions of the fishes.
  • How to interpret and apply concepts like planning, decision-making, and problem-solving in stories.

Simple explanation

In this lesson, we will look at a story about three fishes and see how they think and act in different situations. We will learn how to understand their plans, why they make certain choices, and how to solve problems they face. This helps us develop our thinking skills and understand stories better.

Step-by-step breakdown

1. Identify the problem or situation: Understand what the fishes are facing.

2. Analyze the actions of the fishes: Why did they do what they did?

3. Understand the reasoning behind their decisions: What was their plan?

4. Apply reasoning to similar problems: How can we think like the fishes?

5. Learn from their solutions: What lessons do their actions teach us?

Worked example

Question:

The story mentions three fishes: Anagat Ladhaata, Pratyutpann Malat, and Yadbhala. They are planning to escape from fishermen who want to catch them. Anagat Ladhaata suggests leaving the pond early in the morning before the fishermen arrive. Malat and Yadbhala agree with him.

If you are Malat, what plan would you suggest to avoid being caught? Explain your reasoning.

Solution:

Step 1:

Understand the problem: The fishes need to avoid being caught by fishermen who plan to come early in the morning.

Step 2:

Analyze the plan of Anagat Ladhaata:

  • He suggests leaving the pond before the fishermen arrive.
  • The reason is that if they leave early, they will not be caught in the fishermen's net.

Step 3:

Think about what Malat might suggest:

  • Since Malat agrees with leaving early, her plan would also be to leave the pond before the fishermen come.

Step 4:

Reasoning behind the plan:

  • Leaving early ensures they are safe from the fishermen's trap.
  • The plan is based on timing — acting before the danger arrives.

Step 5:

Conclusion:

  • As Malat, I would suggest that all fishes leave the pond early in the morning, just like Anagat Ladhaata, to stay safe.
  • The reason is that catching the fishermen unawares gives us a better chance to escape.

Final note:

This story teaches us that planning ahead and acting at the right time can help us avoid problems.

Common mistake

Many students might think that hiding or staying still is the best way to avoid being caught. But in stories like this, planning to leave early or at the right moment is more effective because it prevents the danger altogether.

Quick check question

If you were one of the fishes and knew the fishermen would come at dawn, what other plan could you suggest besides leaving early? Why do you think that plan might work or not work?

Summary

In this lesson, we learned how fishes in a story plan to escape from danger by leaving early. We saw how understanding the reasoning behind their actions helps us think better about solving problems. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

📌 RecapSummary

Recap of Chapter 10: तीन मछलियाँ

What you will learn

  • The main ideas and concepts covered in the chapter.
  • How the three different types of मछलियाँ (fish) behave and what lessons they teach.
  • Important vocabulary and their meanings.
  • The moral and practical lessons from the story.

Simple explanation

This chapter tells the story of three different मछलियाँ (fish) living in a beautiful pond. Each fish has a different way of thinking and acting. The story teaches us how their attitudes and actions affect their lives. It also introduces important words and ideas that help us understand their behavior and the moral of the story.

Step-by-step breakdown

1. Introduction to the story: The story begins with a peaceful pond in North India where three fish live happily.

2. Descriptions of the three fish:

  • अनागत लवधाता: Very wise and careful, always thinking ahead.
  • प्रतयुतपनन म लत: Sharp-minded and quick to solve problems.
  • यद ््भलवषय: Lazy and carefree, believes everything will be fine without effort.

3. Their behavior and attitude:

  • The wise fish plans for dangers.
  • The clever fish quickly finds solutions.
  • The lazy fish relies on luck and believes "what will be, will be."

4. The story’s lesson:

  • The fish learn about danger from fishermen.
  • They decide to leave the pond before the fishermen come.
  • The lazy fish refuses to leave, trusting that nothing bad will happen.

5. Outcome:

  • The fishermen come, catch the fish, and the lazy fish gets trapped because it didn’t plan or act.
  • The story teaches the importance of being careful, planning, and not relying solely on luck.

Worked example

Question: Imagine you are one of the fish in the pond. The fishermen are coming tomorrow morning. The wise fish suggests leaving the pond now, but the lazy fish says, "What will happen, will happen." What should you do? Explain your reasoning.

Solution:

  • Step 1: Recognize the danger — fishermen coming tomorrow.
  • Step 2: Think about what the wise fish says — it’s better to leave early to stay safe.
  • Step 3: Consider the lazy fish’s opinion — trusting luck is risky.
  • Step 4: Decide to leave the pond now, just like the wise fish, because it’s safer.
  • Step 5: Understand that planning and acting early help avoid danger.

Conceptual interpretation: Acting early and planning protect us from problems, just like the wise fish. Relying on luck or ignoring danger can lead to trouble.

Common mistake

Many students think that being lazy or trusting luck is okay because nothing bad has happened yet. But the lesson shows that ignoring danger and not planning can lead to problems later. Always think ahead and act wisely.

Quick check question

Imagine you see a storm coming. Would you wait for it to start or find a safe place now? Why?

Summary

In this chapter, we learned about three fish with different attitudes. The story teaches us to be wise, plan ahead, and not rely only on luck. Remember, thinking carefully and acting early can save us from trouble. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

Q: कहानी में तीन मछलियाँ कहाँ रहती हैं?
  • तीन मछलियाँ एक नदी में रहती हैं।
  • नदी का पानी धीरे-धीरे बहता है।
  • मछलियाँ नदी के पानी में तैरती हैं।
  • नदी में अन्य जीव जैसे मछलियाँ और कीड़े भी रहते हैं।
  • मछलियाँ अपनी पूंछ से पानी के तलों पर रगड़ती हैं।
  • नदी के किनारे पेड़ और घास उगती हैं।

Q: सततंभ 'क' और 'ि' में क्या फर्क है?

सततंभ 'क' और 'ि' में फर्क यह है कि 'क' का मतलब है कि कोई शब्द शुरू में 'क' से शुरू होता है, जैसे कि 'कुर्सी', 'कमल'। वहीं, 'ि' का मतलब है कि कोई शब्द में 'इ' स्वर आता है, जैसे कि 'इमली', 'इंद्रधनुष'। दोनों ही अलग-अलग अक्षर हैं और उनके इस्तेमाल से शब्दों का अर्थ बदल जाता है।

Q: प्रतयुतपनन म लत ने मछलियों से क्या उपाय किया?

Q: मछलियों का भय किस कारण था?

Q: सामान्यतः मछलियों का जीवन कैसा होता है?

Q: सततंभ 'क' और 'ि' में क्या अंतर है?

सततंभ 'क' और 'ि' में मुख्य अंतर यह है कि 'क' का इस्तेमाल शब्द के शुरुआत में होता है, जैसे "कल" या "कमल", और 'ि' का इस्तेमाल शब्द के बीच या अंत में होता है, जैसे "बिल्ली" या "सिंह". दोनों ही स्वर हैं, लेकिन उनका इस्तेमाल अलग-अलग जगह होता है। 'क' एक व्यंजन है और 'ि' एक स्वर है जो "इ" की आवाज देता है।

Q: मछलियों ने अपने उपाय क्यों चुने?

📖 11. हमारे ये कलामंदिर

🎯 What We LearnIntroduction

What We Learn in Chapter 11: हमारे ये कलामंदिर

1. What you will learn

In this lesson, you will learn about the beautiful ancient caves and monuments like Ajanta and Ellora. You will understand how these caves were made, what kind of pictures and sculptures are inside them, and why they are important for our history and culture. You will also learn how these caves tell us stories from long ago and how they are preserved today.

2. Simple explanation

Ajanta and Ellora are famous caves in India. They are very old and have many pictures and sculptures inside. These caves were built by skilled artists and craftsmen hundreds of years ago. The pictures show stories from religion, nature, and everyday life. These caves are called "Kalamandir" because they are like temples made by art and sculpture. They help us understand how people lived and believed in the past. Today, these caves are treasures of our country and need to be preserved carefully.

3. Step-by-step breakdown

  • Introduction to Ajanta and Ellora: These are ancient caves in India, carved out of rocks.
  • Why they are special: They contain beautiful paintings and sculptures that tell stories from history and religion.
  • How they were made: Skilled artists carved these caves from solid rocks using tools and their imagination.
  • What is inside: Pictures of gods, goddesses, animals, and scenes from daily life.
  • Importance: These caves show our rich cultural heritage and artistic skills of ancient times.
  • Preservation: We must take care of these caves by not damaging them and respecting their beauty.

4. Worked example

Question: The following data shows the distance from a city to two famous caves: Ajanta is 40 kilometers away, and Ellora is 70 kilometers away. Draw a simple bar graph to compare the distances of these caves from the city.

Step 1: Draw two vertical bars on a graph paper.

Step 2: Label the first bar as "Ajanta" and the second as "Ellora".

Step 3: Mark 40 units on the vertical axis for Ajanta and 70 units for Ellora, representing the distances in kilometers.

Step 4: Fill the bars up to these marks.

Step 5: Observe which bar is taller to compare the distances.

Reasoning: Bar graphs help us easily compare quantities like distances. The taller the bar, the greater the value.

Conceptual interpretation: Ellora is farther from the city than Ajanta, which can help in planning visits or understanding the spread of ancient sites.

5. Common mistake

Many students forget to label the axes properly or confuse the scale. Always remember to mark the scale clearly and label what each bar represents. Without labels, the graph becomes confusing.

6. Quick check question

Why are Ajanta and Ellora called "Kalamandir"? What stories do their pictures tell us?

7. Summary

In this lesson, we learned about Ajanta and Ellora caves, their beautiful paintings and sculptures, and their importance for our history. These caves are a part of our cultural heritage and need to be preserved. Next, you can explore more about how artists created these caves and what stories they wanted to tell through their art.

Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

Q: अध्याय के अनुसार हमारे समाज के लिए 'कलामंदिर' का क्या महत्व है?
  • 'कलामंदिर' समाज में सांस्कृतिक एकता और समरसता को बढ़ावा देता है。
  • यह विभिन्न समुदायों के बीच संवाद और सहिष्णुता को प्रोत्साहित करता है。
  • कलामंदिर के माध्यम से युवाओं को रचनात्मकता और आत्म-अभिव्यक्ति का मंच मिलता है。
  • यह समाज के शैक्षणिक और मनोरंजक जीवन को संतुलित बनाता है。
  • कलामंदिर के उपक्रमों से रोजगार और उद्यमिता के नए अवसर पैदा होते हैं。
  • अंततः, कलामंदिर हमें हमारी परंपराओं और समकालीन नवाचारों को जोड़ने की दिशा देता है。

Q: कौन-कौन सी सांस्कृतिक श्रेणियों के बारे में इस अध्याय में चर्चा की गई है?
  • संगीत की प्राचीन विधाएँ जैसे सितार, तबला, वायलिन, और बाँसुरी पर रोशनी डाली गई है。
  • नृत्य में भरतनाट्यम, कथक, कुश्ती और लैंगिक नृत्य जैसे प्रकारों का उल्लेख है。
  • नाटक के क्षेत्र में रंगमंच की मूल अवधारणा और प्राचीन नाट्य शैली का वर्णन है。
  • चित्रकला की श्रेणी में पारंपरिक पेंटिंग, भित्ति चित्र और कैरिकेचर का परिचय है。
  • साहित्यिक खंड में उपन्यास, कविता, कहानी और गद्य तथा उनके विशिष्ट विषयों पर चर्चा है。
  • धार्मिक और पौराणिक कथाओं के माध्यम से सांस्कृतिक आदर्शों को समझने का प्रयास किया गया है。

🧪 Worked ExamplesExamples

What You Will Learn

Today, we will learn how to interpret and answer questions based on pictures and descriptions of caves and their features, as given in the chapter "हमारे ये कलामंदिर". We will focus on understanding how to read descriptions, interpret pictures, and answer related questions accurately.

Simple Explanation

In this chapter, we learn about ancient caves like Ajanta and Ellora, their artwork, and their features. We see pictures and descriptions of these caves, which tell us about their size, shape, and the art inside. To answer questions, we need to carefully read the descriptions and look at the pictures, then use that information to find the answers.

Step-by-Step Breakdown

1. Read the description carefully: Understand what features or facts are being described about the caves.

2. Look at the pictures or diagrams: Observe the details like size, shape, or specific features mentioned.

3. Match the description with the picture: Find the part of the picture that matches the description.

4. Answer the questions based on the matching: Use logical reasoning to answer questions about size, shape, or features.

5. Check your answers: Make sure your answers match the details given in the description and the picture.

Worked Example

Question:

The description says, "In the caves, paintings on the walls are very detailed and look like they are about to speak." Based on this, answer:

a) What is special about the paintings?

b) Why do you think they look like they are about to speak?

Step 1:

Read the description carefully. It tells us the paintings are very detailed and lively.

Step 2:

Look at the picture of the cave paintings. Notice how the figures are drawn with fine lines and expressive faces.

Step 3:

Match the description with the picture. The detailed and lively paintings are on the walls.

Step 4:

Answer:

a) The paintings are very detailed and look lively.

b) Because the artists drew the faces and gestures very carefully, making them seem like they are about to speak.

Step 5:

Check if the answers match the description and picture. Yes, they do.

Common Mistake

A common mistake is to look only at the picture without reading the description carefully. This can lead to incorrect answers, especially if the picture is confusing or incomplete.

Quick Check Question

Look at the description: "The caves have large, tall entrances, and inside, the walls are covered with colorful paintings."

Based on this, answer:

What features do the caves have, and what is inside them?

Summary

Today, you learned how to read descriptions and look at pictures of caves to answer questions accurately. Remember to carefully read the details and match them with the pictures. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

Q: इस अध्याय में वर्णित कलामंदिर के प्रमुख कला प्रकार कौन से हैं?

📌 RecapSummary

1. What you will learn

In this lesson, we will learn about how colors are made using natural things like plants and how we can create different colors ourselves using simple ingredients. We will understand how colors in paintings and pictures are made naturally and practically.

2. Simple explanation

Colors can be made from nature, like from plants and flowers. We can also make colors at home using things like leaves, flowers, or spices. These colors are used in paintings, drawings, and decorations. Making colors from natural things is easy and safe, and it helps us understand how colors are created in nature.

3. Step-by-step breakdown

  • Understanding natural colors:

Nature provides many colors through plants, flowers, and fruits. For example, spinach and coriander give green, turmeric gives yellow, and tea leaves give brown.

  • Making green color:

Take some spinach or coriander leaves, wash them well, and then grind them into a paste. Mix this paste with a little water to make green color for painting.

  • Making yellow color:

Use turmeric powder. Mix a small amount with water to get a bright yellow color.

  • Making brown color:

Take some tea leaves, boil them in water, and then filter the water. The resulting brownish water can be used as a natural brown color.

  • Using colors in art:

These natural colors can be used in drawings, paintings, or decorations. They are safe and eco-friendly.

  • Why natural colors are important:

They are made from safe ingredients, do not harm the environment, and help us learn about nature’s colors.

4. Worked example

Suppose you want to make green, yellow, and brown colors at home for a painting.

  • Green:

Take some fresh spinach leaves. Wash them thoroughly.

Grind the leaves into a smooth paste using a mortar and pestle or blender.

Mix this paste with a little water to make a green color.

Use this in your painting.

  • Yellow:

Take a small amount of turmeric powder.

Mix it with water to make a bright yellow color.

Use this in your artwork.

  • Brown:

Boil some tea leaves in water for a few minutes.

Filter the liquid to remove the leaves.

The brownish water is your natural brown color for painting.

This way, you have made three different colors using natural ingredients.

5. Common mistake

Many children think that colors can only be made from artificial paints or chemicals. But in reality, nature provides many colors, and they can be easily made at home using plants, spices, and other natural things.

6. Quick check question

What natural ingredient can you use to make yellow color at home?

---

7. Summary

In this lesson, we learned how to make colors from natural things like leaves, turmeric, and tea. These colors are safe, eco-friendly, and easy to prepare at home. Remember, nature is full of colors, and we can use simple ingredients to create beautiful colors for our art and crafts.

Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

Q: Why do paintings on walls and papers look different?

Paintings on walls and papers look different because they are made on different surfaces. Walls are rough and big, so paintings there are usually bigger and may have thicker colors. Papers are smooth and small, so paintings on papers are more detailed and neat. The material of the surface affects how the colors look and how easy it is to make the painting.

Wall paintings are permanent and can be seen by everyone, while paper paintings are portable and can be kept in albums or frames.

Q: How do caves protect the paintings inside them?

Caves protect the paintings inside them because they are dark, cool, and stay covered with rocks. These conditions help keep the paintings safe from sunlight, rain, and wind, which can damage them. The thick walls of the caves also prevent dust and dirt from reaching the paintings, so they stay preserved for a long time.

Q: What is the difference between Ajanta and Ellora caves?

Ajanta and Ellora caves are two famous caves in India, but they are different in many ways. Ajanta caves are known for their beautiful paintings and sculptures that show stories from Buddha's life. They are mostly Buddhist caves. Ellora caves, on the other hand, have not only Buddhist caves but also Hindu and Jain caves, so they show different religions. Ellora caves are also bigger and have amazing rock carvings and temples. So, the main difference is that Ajanta has mostly Buddhist paintings, while Ellora has Buddhist, Hindu, and Jain caves with big carvings.

Q: How are paintings on paper different from those on walls?

On the other hand, paintings on walls are big and are made directly on the wall surface. They are permanent and can be seen by many people at once. So, paper paintings are small and portable, while wall paintings are large and permanent.

📖 12. गंगा की कहानी
✅ RAG (14 chunks)LKB: ✅DKB: 25 entries
✅RAG source material found — 14 chunks indexed from textbook PDF.

🎯 What We LearnIntroduction

What You Will Learn

In this lesson, we will understand how the Ganga river is connected to different places, its journey from the mountains to the sea, and how human activities affect it. We will also learn about the importance of keeping the river clean and how we can help.

Simple Explanation

The Ganga is a very important river in India. It starts in the mountains called the Himalayas and flows down through many cities and towns until it reaches the ocean. Along the way, many people live near it, and they use its water for drinking, bathing, and farming. But sometimes, factories and cities dump dirty waste into the river, which makes it dirty and harmful. Now, people are trying to keep the Ganga clean so that everyone can enjoy its pure water.

Step-by-Step Breakdown

  • The Ganga begins in the Himalayas, in a place called the Himalaya mountain range.
  • It flows through different regions, passing towns and cities like Haridwar, Kanpur, and Kolkata.
  • The river joins other smaller rivers and streams along its journey.
  • It finally reaches the sea, carrying water, sediments, and sometimes pollution.
  • Many people depend on the Ganga for their daily needs.
  • Human activities like factories, urban waste, and bathing can pollute the river.
  • Efforts are being made to clean the Ganga, such as stopping waste from factories and planting trees along its banks.
  • We can help by not throwing garbage into the river and saving water.

Worked Example

Suppose the Ganga starts in the Himalayas and flows through three places: Haridwar, Kanpur, and Kolkata, before reaching the ocean.

  • The total journey is from the mountains to the sea.
  • Along the way, it passes through towns where many people use its water.
  • If factories dump waste into the river in Kanpur, the water becomes dirty.
  • To keep the water clean, factories must treat their waste before releasing it.
  • If everyone does their part, the Ganga will stay pure and healthy.

Common Mistake

Many students think that pollution only comes from factories. But pollution also happens when people throw garbage into the river or wash clothes with soap. It’s important to remember that everyone’s actions affect the river’s health.

Quick Check Question

Where does the Ganga river start, and where does it end? Why is it important to keep it clean?

Summary

The Ganga river begins in the Himalayas and flows to the ocean. It passes through many towns where people depend on it. Pollution from human activities makes the river dirty, but efforts are being made to keep it clean. We all can help by not polluting the river and saving water. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

🧪 Worked ExamplesExamples

Lesson on Worked Examples from "गंगा की कहानी"

What you will learn

Today, we will learn how to solve a typical question based on the story of the Ganga river, focusing on understanding the flow of water, pollution, and related concepts. We will see how to analyze data, interpret information, and answer questions logically and scientifically.

Simple explanation

In this lesson, we will look at a story about the Ganga river and learn how to answer questions based on the story. The questions may ask about the sources of pollution, the importance of clean water, or the effects of pollution. We will learn how to carefully read the question, understand what it asks, and then find the answer step-by-step based on the story and facts.

Step-by-step breakdown

1. Read the question carefully.

2. Identify what information is given in the question.

3. Recall the relevant facts from the story or scientific concepts.

4. Think about how the facts connect to the question.

5. Write the answer logically, explaining each step clearly.

6. Check if the answer makes sense with the story or facts.

Worked example

Question:

The story says that the Ganga river gets polluted because factories and cities dump waste into it. If the pollution increases, what will happen to the water of the Ganga?

a) The water will become clean.

b) The water will become dirty and harmful.

c) The water will turn into ice.

d) The water will flow faster.

Step 1:

Identify what the question asks: It asks about the effect of increased pollution on the Ganga river water.

Step 2:

Recall the facts: Pollution from factories and cities makes water dirty and harmful. Pollution is the addition of waste and harmful substances.

Step 3:

Connect facts to options:

  • Option a) says water will become clean — wrong, pollution makes water dirty.
  • Option b) says water will become dirty and harmful — correct, based on facts.
  • Option c) says water will turn into ice — unrelated to pollution.
  • Option d) says flow will increase — not directly related to pollution.

Step 4:

Choose the correct answer: b) The water will become dirty and harmful.

Step 5:

Explain why: Pollution adds waste to water, making it dirty and unsafe for humans and animals.

Final note:

When pollution increases, the water quality worsens, which can cause health problems for people and harm aquatic life.

Common mistake

Many students might think pollution makes water cleaner or has no effect. Remember, pollution always harms water quality, making it unsafe.

Quick check question

If factories stop dumping waste into the Ganga, what will happen to the water?

a) It will become more polluted.

b) It will become cleaner.

c) It will turn into steam.

d) It will flow backward.

Summary

In this lesson, we learned how to analyze questions about the Ganga river, based on the story and scientific facts. We practiced understanding pollution and its effects on water quality. Remember to read the question carefully, connect it with facts, and answer logically. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

📌 RecapSummary

1. What you will learn

In this lesson, we will review the key points about the story of the Ganga River, its importance, pollution issues, and how we can protect it. We will also understand how the river's story connects to our environment and daily life.

2. Simple explanation

The Ganga is a very important river in India. It starts from the Himalayas and flows through many towns and cities, providing water for millions of people. Over time, pollution from factories, cities, and other human activities has made the water dirty and unsafe. Now, people are working to keep the Ganga clean and pure again. This story teaches us how rivers are connected to our lives and how we can help protect them.

3. Step-by-step breakdown

  • The Ganga originates from the Himalayas, in the region called "Himalaya" or "Himadri."
  • It flows through different places like Haridwar, Kanpur, and Kolkata, providing water for drinking, agriculture, and industry.
  • Many factories and cities along the river dump waste into it, polluting the water.
  • Pollution makes the water dirty, affecting animals, plants, and people who depend on it.
  • The government and people have started efforts to clean the Ganga, such as stopping factory waste and planting trees.
  • The story of the Ganga teaches us the importance of keeping our rivers clean for future generations.

4. Worked example

Suppose a factory near the Ganga dumps waste into the river, making the water dirty. To solve this problem:

  • First, identify the source of pollution (the factory waste).
  • Then, stop or treat the waste before it enters the river.
  • Next, plant trees along the riverbank to prevent soil erosion and filter pollutants.
  • Finally, educate people about not throwing waste into the river.

This way, the water remains clean and safe for everyone.

5. Common mistake

Many students think that pollution only happens in big cities or factories. But pollution can happen anywhere—small towns, villages, or even households. Every person can help by not throwing waste into rivers and keeping them clean.

6. Quick check question

Where does the Ganga River originate from, and why is it important to keep it clean?

7. Summary

Today, we learned about the story of the Ganga River, its origin, journey, and the pollution it faces. We also understood how pollution affects the river and what we can do to protect it. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

Q: What is the role of people in protecting Ganga?

People play an important role in protecting Ganga by keeping it clean and not polluting it. They should avoid throwing garbage or harmful chemicals into the river. Instead, people can plant trees along the riverbank and use water wisely. Everyone's efforts help keep Ganga pure and beautiful for future generations.

Q: How does pollution impact aquatic life in rivers?

Pollution in rivers makes the water dirty and harmful for fish and other aquatic animals. When factories and people throw waste into the river, it can kill the fish and make it difficult for them to live. Clean water is very important for aquatic life to survive and stay healthy. If the river is polluted, many animals that live in the water may die or leave the river.

Q: What is the significance of the city Varanasi in the chapter?

In the chapter "गंगा की कहानी," Varanasi is very important because it is one of the main places where the Ganga River flows. Varanasi is a famous city in India that is known for its spiritual significance and beautiful ghats (steps by the river). It is also called the city of Lord Shiva and is a place where many people come to take holy baths in the Ganga River to purify their souls. So, Varanasi helps us understand the importance of the Ganga River in people's religious and cultural life.

Q: How does pollution from cities affect the river Ganga?

Pollution from cities affects the river Ganga by making its water dirty and harmful. When factories and people throw garbage, chemicals, and waste into the river, it pollutes the water. This can harm fish, plants, and animals living in the river, and it also makes the water unsafe for people to use. So, keeping the river clean is very important to protect Ganga and all the life in it.

📖 2. न्याय की कु सी

🎯 What We LearnIntroduction

What We Learn: Justice and Fairness

1. What you will learn

Today, we will learn about the importance of justice and fairness in our daily life. We will understand how justice helps us solve problems fairly and why it is necessary for everyone to be treated equally.

2. Simple explanation

Justice means giving everyone what they deserve or what is right. Fairness is about treating everyone equally and kindly. When we follow justice, everyone gets a fair chance, and problems are solved peacefully. Justice helps us decide what is right and wrong, and it makes sure no one is treated unfairly.

3. Step-by-step breakdown

  • What is justice? Justice is about fairness and doing what is right. It helps us decide how to treat others and solve conflicts.
  • Why is justice important? Justice keeps peace and harmony. It prevents fights and makes sure everyone is happy and safe.
  • How do we practice justice? By listening to both sides of a story, being honest, and treating everyone equally.
  • Example from story: In the story of Raraa and the children playing, justice was shown when Raraa wanted to find out who was right and who was wrong. He listened carefully and then decided what was fair.

4. Worked example

Suppose two children are fighting over a toy. One child says, "I had it first," and the other says, "I want to play with it now." How can justice help here?

  • First, listen to both children carefully.
  • Then, decide what is fair. Maybe, they can take turns playing.
  • If needed, the teacher or parent can help decide who gets the toy first.
  • This way, both children are treated fairly, and the problem is solved peacefully.

5. Common mistake

Many children think that justice means always giving the biggest or strongest person what they want. But actually, justice is about fairness and doing what is right for everyone, not just for one person.

6. Quick check question

Imagine two friends are fighting over a ball. One says, "It's mine," and the other says, "No, I found it first." What should you do to be fair?

  • Think about listening to both friends and deciding what is right.

7. Summary

Today, we learned that justice is about fairness and doing what is right. It helps us solve problems peacefully and treat everyone equally. Remember to listen carefully, be honest, and treat everyone fairly. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

🧪 Worked ExamplesExamples

Lesson on "Worked Examples" in Justice (न्याय की कुसी) for Grade 5

What you will learn

  • How to analyze and understand a story or situation to find justice or fairness.
  • How to identify the key events and their importance.
  • How to think critically about fairness and justice in real-life situations.
  • How to solve questions related to justice stories step-by-step.

Simple explanation

In stories about justice, we learn how people or characters behave, what is right or wrong, and how fairness is achieved. When we look at a story, we find clues about what is fair and unfair. By analyzing these clues, we can understand what should be done to keep justice.

Step-by-step breakdown

1. Read the story carefully.

2. Identify the main characters and what they did.

3. Find out what was fair or unfair in the story.

4. Think about what should be done to make things right.

5. Use clues from the story to answer questions.

Worked example

Question:

In the story, Raraa (ररा्रा) and other children were playing a game where they pretended to be kings and queens. Raraa wanted to sit on the throne because he thought he deserved it. The teacher told the children that only those who are honest and fair can sit on the throne. Raraa remembered that he had lied earlier and felt he was not deserving. What should Raraa do to be fair and just? Explain your answer.

---

Step 1: Understand the story

  • Raraa wanted to sit on the throne.
  • The teacher said only honest children can sit on the throne.
  • Raraa remembered he lied earlier.

Step 2: Find the key point

  • Fairness means being honest and truthful.
  • Raraa’s mistake was lying, which is unfair.

Step 3: What should Raraa do?

  • Raraa should admit his mistake and tell the truth.
  • Only then will he be fair and deserving to sit on the throne.

Step 4: Why?

  • Because honesty is a key part of justice.
  • Admitting mistakes shows fairness and truthfulness.

Final answer:

Raraa should honestly admit his mistake and tell the truth. This will make him fair and deserving to sit on the throne.

---

Analysis of the Example:

  • The story teaches that justice is based on honesty.
  • Admitting mistakes is a way to restore fairness.
  • Justice is not just about getting what you want, but doing what is right.

Common mistake

Many students think that just sitting on the throne or getting what they want is justice. But true justice is about being honest, fair, and truthful. Admitting mistakes and acting rightly are the real ways to achieve justice.

Quick check question

Imagine you took your friend’s pencil without asking. Later, you realize it was wrong. What should you do to be fair?

Think about honesty and fairness in your answer.

Summary

  • Justice involves honesty, fairness, and truthfulness.
  • To be just, admit mistakes and act rightly.
  • Stories teach us how to behave fairly in real life.
  • Practice analyzing stories carefully to find what is fair and unfair.

Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

📌 RecapSummary

Lesson on "Recap of Justice" (न्याय की कुसी)

1. What you will learn

Today, we will review what justice means through stories and examples from Indian culture and stories. We will understand how justice is important in resolving conflicts, making fair decisions, and maintaining peace in society.

2. Simple explanation

Justice means fairness. It is about making sure everyone is treated equally and rightly. In stories, justice is shown when wrongdoers are punished and good people are rewarded. In real life, justice helps to solve problems fairly so that everyone feels happy and safe.

3. Step-by-step breakdown

  • Stories and examples: The stories from Indian culture, like tales of kings and common people, show us how justice was served.
  • Conflict resolution: Justice helps solve fights and disagreements fairly.
  • Fair decision-making: Leaders and judges decide what is right and wrong.
  • Importance in society: Justice keeps peace and happiness in communities.
  • Real-life application: When someone cheats or hurts others, justice helps to punish the wrongdoer and protect the victim.

4. Worked example

Imagine two children, Ramu and Shyam, both want the same toy. Ramu takes the toy without asking. How should justice be applied here?

  • First, understand that taking without asking is unfair.
  • The teacher or parent should listen to both children.
  • If Ramu took the toy unfairly, he should return it and apologize.
  • Shyam should be given the toy back.
  • This way, fairness is restored, and both children learn what is right.

This example shows how justice helps in solving conflicts fairly and teaches us to treat everyone equally.

5. Common mistake

Many students think justice only means punishment. But justice also means fairness, kindness, and understanding. It’s about doing what is right, not just punishing wrong.

6. Quick check question

Imagine a story where a king helps a poor man get his stolen money back. Why is this an example of justice?

(Answer: Because the king is making sure the poor man gets his right and punishing the thief, which is fair and just.)

7. Summary

Justice is about fairness and doing what is right. It helps solve problems, punish wrongdoers, and reward good behavior. Remember, justice keeps society peaceful and happy. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

Q: Why is it important to identify 'दिशेषण' in sentences?

It is important to identify 'दिशेषण' (adjectives) in sentences because they tell us more about the nouns (like people, places, or things). They help us understand how something looks, feels, or is, making our sentences more clear and interesting. For example, in the sentence "बड़ा घर," the word "बड़ा" (big) is a दिशेषण that tells us about the size of the घर (house). Knowing the दिशेषण helps us imagine the sentence better!

Q: What are the different types of 'दिशेषण' mentioned in the chapter?

इस अध्याय में 'दिशेषण' के तीन मुख्य प्रकार बताए गए हैं:

1. स्थान दर्शक दिशेषण (जैसे, ऊपर, नीचे, यहाँ, वहाँ) – यह बताता है कि कोई वस्तु या व्यक्ति कहाँ है।

2. समय दर्शक दिशेषण (जैसे, आज, कल, अभी, पहले) – यह बताता है कि कोई काम कब हुआ या होगा।

3. गुण दर्शक दिशेषण (जैसे, अच्छा, बहुत, तेज़, सुन्दर) – यह बताता है कि कोई वस्तु या व्यक्ति कैसा है।

ये तीनों प्रकार हमें किसी बात को और अच्छी तरह समझाने में मदद करते हैं।

Q: What is the difference between 'सकारात्मक' and 'नकारात्मक' न्याय?

📖 3. चाँद का कु रता

🎯 What We LearnIntroduction

What We Learn: चाँद का कु रता

1. What you will learn

  • आप जानेंगे कि चाँद का आकार कैसे बदलता है और क्यों।
  • आप समझेंगे कि चाँद अपने आकार को कैसे बदलता है।
  • आप सीखेंगे कि चाँद का आकार बदलने का कारण क्या है।
  • आप जानेंगे कि चाँद का आकार बदलने से हमें क्या समझ में आता है।

2. Simple explanation

चाँद का आकार हर रात अलग-अलग दिखता है। कभी वह छोटा होता है, कभी बड़ा। इसे हम चाँद का "कु रता" कहते हैं। यह आकार बदलने का कारण है कि चाँद सूर्य की रोशनी को कैसे देखता है। जब चाँद सूर्य की तरफ होता है, तो वह पूरा चमकता है और बड़ा दिखता है। जब वह सूर्य से दूर होता है, तो वह छोटा दिखता है। यह बदलाव हमें चाँद की स्थिति के बारे में भी जानकारी देता है।

3. Step-by-step breakdown

  • चाँद का आकार क्यों बदलता है? क्योंकि चाँद सूर्य की रोशनी को अलग-अलग कोण से देखता है।
  • चाँद का आकार कब बड़ा होता है? जब चाँद सूर्य के सामने होता है या उसके करीब होता है।
  • चाँद का आकार कब छोटा होता है? जब चाँद सूर्य से दूर होता है।
  • यह बदलाव क्यों जरूरी है? क्योंकि इससे हमें पता चलता है कि चाँद कहां है और उसकी स्थिति क्या है।
  • हम चाँद का आकार कैसे देख सकते हैं? रात को आकाश में देख कर।

4. Worked example

मान लीजिए कि रात को आप आकाश में देखते हैं कि चाँद बड़ा दिख रहा है। इसका मतलब है कि चाँद सूर्य के बहुत करीब है। यदि अगले दिन आप देखते हैं कि चाँद छोटा हो गया है, तो इसका मतलब है कि चाँद सूर्य से दूर हो गया है। इस तरह, चाँद का आकार उसकी स्थिति का संकेत देता है।

5. Common mistake

लोग सोचते हैं कि चाँद का आकार हर रात अलग-अलग होता है क्योंकि वह अपने आप बदलता है। लेकिन असल में, यह आकार बदलता नहीं है, बल्कि हम उसे अलग-अलग तरह से देखते हैं क्योंकि उसकी स्थिति बदलती रहती है।

6. Quick check question

चाँद का आकार हर रात क्यों बदलता है?

  • क्योंकि चाँद अपने आकार को खुद बदलता है।
  • क्योंकि सूर्य की रोशनी का कोण बदलता है।
  • क्योंकि चाँद अपनी जगह से हिल जाता है।
  • क्योंकि मौसम बदलता है।

7. Summary

  • चाँद का आकार हर रात अलग दिखता है क्योंकि उसकी स्थिति सूर्य और पृथ्वी के बीच बदलती रहती है।
  • जब चाँद सूर्य के सामने होता है, तो वह बड़ा दिखता है।
  • जब वह सूर्य से दूर होता है, तो वह छोटा दिखता है।
  • यह बदलाव हमें चाँद की स्थिति और उसकी चाल के बारे में जानकारी देता है।
  • अब आप आकाश में चाँद को देखकर उसकी स्थिति का अंदाजा लगा सकते हैं।

अगले कदम के लिए, इन बातों का अभ्यास करें और अपने आस-पास के आकाश को ध्यान से देखें।

Q: चाँद के कौन-कौन से चरण होते हैं, और वे क्यों बनते हैं?
  • चाँद के चार प्रमुख चरण होते हैं: अमावस्या, शुक्ल पक्ष का आधा चंद्र (पक्ष), पूर्णिमा, और कृष्ण पक्ष का आधा चंद्र।
  • ये चरण चाँद के सूर्य से दूरी के कोण के अनुसार बनते हैं, जब चाँद का प्रकाशित हिस्सा पृथ्वी से देखा जाता है।
  • अमावस्या पर चाँद और सूर्य एक ही दिशा में होते हैं, इसलिए प्रकाशित हिस्सा पृथ्वी से दूर होता है।
  • पूर्णिमा पर चाँद सूर्य के विपरीत होता है, जिससे प्रकाशित हिस्सा पूरी तरह से पृथ्वी की ओर रहता है।
  • शुक्ल पक्ष और कृष्ण पक्ष का आधा चंद्र क्रम में प्रकाश के आधे-आधे हिस्से देखकर बनते हैं।
  • चाँद के चरणों के बीच के बदलाव को चंद्र मास या महीने के रूप में जाना जाता है।

Q: चाँद के आकार के कारण उसे प्रकाश में परिवर्तन क्यों दिखाई देता है?

Q: चंद्र ग्रहण और सूर्य ग्रहण में क्या अंतर है, और चंद्र ग्रहण कौन-से चरण में दिखाई देता है?

  • चंद्र ग्रहण केवल पूर्णिमा के दौरान ही हो सकता है क्योंकि वह समय चंद्रमा और सूर्य की रेखा में होते हैं।

  • इस लाल रंग के प्रभाव को लैगुन (Lunar eclipse) कहा जाता है।

🧪 Worked ExamplesExamples

Lesson on Worked Examples for "चाँद का कु रता"

1. What you will learn

Today, we will learn how to solve examples related to the changing shape of the moon, specifically how to understand and describe the moon's different shapes or phases, and how to use clues from the story to identify the correct answers.

2. Simple explanation

In the story, the moon is described as changing shape every day. These changes are called "phases" of the moon. By looking at clues like the moon's size, shape, or the words used to describe it, we can figure out what phase the moon is in. We will learn how to analyze these clues step-by-step to find the correct answer.

3. Step-by-step breakdown

  • Identify the question: Understand what the question is asking about, such as the shape of the moon or the words used to describe it.
  • Read the clues carefully: Look for words like "small," "big," "growing," "shrinking," or descriptions of shape.
  • Recall moon phases: Remember that the moon goes through different phases, like new moon, crescent, half, full moon, and so on.
  • Match clues to phases: Compare the clues with the characteristics of each moon phase.
  • Choose the correct answer: Pick the option that best fits the clues.

4. Worked example

Let's analyze one of the questions from the story:

Question:

In the story, the moon says, "Sometimes I am small, sometimes I am big." Which phase of the moon does this describe?

Step 1:

The moon's size changes from small to big.

Step 2:

Remember the moon phases:

  • New moon: not visible, very small
  • Crescent: small and curved
  • Half moon: half visible
  • Full moon: fully visible and big

Step 3:

Since the moon is sometimes small and sometimes big, it is changing size, which happens during the transition from new moon to full moon.

Step 4:

The phrase "sometimes small, sometimes big" indicates the moon is in the process of changing from a new moon to a full moon or vice versa.

Answer:

The phase described is "Waxing or Waning Moon" (growing or shrinking moon).

In options, this might be represented as "crescent" or "half moon" depending on the clues.

Conceptual reason:

The moon's size appears to change because of its position relative to the Earth and Sun, which causes different visible shapes.

5. Common mistake

Students often think the moon is only full or only new. Remember, the moon's phases are a continuous cycle, and the changing size and shape are natural parts of this cycle.

6. Quick check question

If the moon is described as "shrinking" and "getting smaller," which moon phase is it in?

7. Summary

In this lesson, we learned how to analyze clues about the moon's changing shape and size to identify its phases. Remember to match descriptive words with the characteristics of moon phases. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

📌 RecapSummary

1. What you will learn

In this lesson, we will revisit the main ideas about the moon's changing shape, how the moon's size appears to change, and how the weather and seasons affect the moon and our surroundings. We will also understand how to identify different parts of sentences and how to describe the moon's shape and size changes clearly.

2. Simple explanation

The moon looks different at different times because of how it moves and how the sunlight falls on it. Sometimes, it looks big and round, and other times it looks small or like a thin crescent. The weather and seasons also change how we see the moon and the sky. By observing these changes carefully, we can understand many natural phenomena.

3. Step-by-step breakdown

  • The moon's shape changes because of its position relative to the Earth and Sun. When the moon is between the Sun and Earth, we see a new moon; when it is on the opposite side, we see a full moon.
  • The moon appears to grow larger or smaller because of its orbit around the Earth, which makes it seem like its size is changing.
  • The weather affects how we see the moon. For example, in winter, cold weather can make the air clear, so the moon looks brighter and clearer.
  • The words used to describe the moon's changing size include "big," "small," "growing," and "shrinking."
  • Sentences in the chapter often include words like "एक अ ंगुल-भर चौड ा" (a full circle), "फ ु ट मोटा" (thick), and "दक सी दद न छोटा" (small crescent) to describe the moon's shape.
  • When describing weather, words like "सन-सन चलती हवा" (whistling wind) and "भाडे का क ु रता" (cloudy weather) are used.

4. Worked example

Let's take the sentence: "चाँद एक द द न, माता से यह बोला, 'दसलवा दो माँ, म ुझे ऊन का मोटा एक दझंगोला।'"

  • Here, the moon says to its mother, "Give me a thick woolen blanket."
  • The moon's shape is described as "एक द द न" (a circle), which is full and round.
  • The word "मोटा" (thick) describes the blanket, showing how weather affects the moon's appearance.
  • When we observe the moon, we notice how its shape and size change, just like in this story.

5. Common mistake

Many students think the moon's shape changes because it moves closer or farther from Earth. But actually, the shape change is due to the angle of sunlight and our view, not the moon's distance. Remember, the moon's orbit is almost circular, so its distance from Earth doesn't change enough to affect its size visibly.

6. Quick check question

What words would you use to describe a moon that looks like a thin curve in the sky?

a) Full circle

b) Thick circle

c) Small crescent

d) Big round

(Answer: c) Small crescent

7. Summary

Today, we revisited how the moon's shape and size appear to change because of its position and sunlight. We learned how weather and seasons influence our view of the moon and sky. Remember to observe carefully and use descriptive words like "full," "small," "thick," and "crescent" to describe the moon. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

Q: Why does the moon feel cold in winter?

The moon feels cold in winter because it is very far from the Earth and does not have its own heat. During winter, the temperature on Earth drops, and since the moon is in space, it also becomes very cold. The moon does not have air or water to keep it warm, so it feels cold, especially when the weather is cold on Earth.

Q: Why can't the moon keep a blanket permanently?

The moon can't keep a blanket permanently because it is a big, rocky space object, not a living thing. It doesn't have feelings or needs like us. The moon's size and shape change because of how sunlight hits it and how it moves in the sky. So, it can't keep a blanket like we do to stay warm.

Q: Is it true that the moon's size remains the same all the time?

Q: What is the difference between a thick blanket and a thin one in the story?

In the story, a thick blanket (ऊन का मोटा दझंगोला) keeps the moon warm and cozy during the cold winter, while a thin blanket (अंगुल-भर चौड ा) is too small and not enough to cover the moon properly. The thick blanket is big and warm, but the thin one is small and doesn't give enough warmth or cover.

Q: How does the moon's size change from day to night?

The moon's size changes because it looks bigger or smaller in the sky at different times. Sometimes, it appears large, and sometimes it looks small. This happens because of how the moon is positioned in the sky and how our eyes see it, not because the moon itself is changing size. The moon's shape also changes, like from a full moon to a half moon, but its actual size stays the same.

We can relate the moon's changing shape to real life by thinking about how things change over time. Just like the moon looks different every night — sometimes full, sometimes a thin crescent — many things in our life change too, like our age, our skills, or the seasons. These changes help us understand that change is a natural part of life, and we should be patient and adaptable, just like we wait for the moon to become full again.

📖 4. साङके न
✅ RAG (10 chunks)LKB: ✅DKB: 25 entries
✅RAG source material found — 10 chunks indexed from textbook PDF.

🎯 What We LearnIntroduction

What You Will Learn

  • What are सांकेतिक न (symbolic signs) and their importance.
  • How symbols are used to represent ideas, objects, or actions.
  • Examples of common symbols used in everyday life and in communication.

Simple Explanation

सांकेतिक न यानी कि ऐसे चिन्ह या संकेत जो किसी वस्तु, कार्य या विचार को दर्शाने के लिए प्रयोग किए जाते हैं। ये संकेत हमें जल्दी और आसानी से जानकारी देने का काम करते हैं। जैसे सड़क पर ट्रैफिक संकेत, खेल के निशान, या कंप्यूटर के आइकॉन। ये सब सांकेतिक न हैं, जो बिना शब्दों के बात समझाते हैं।

Step-by-Step Breakdown

1. परिभाषा: सांकेतिक न वे चिन्ह या संकेत हैं जो किसी वस्तु, क्रिया या विचार का प्रतिनिधित्व करते हैं।

2. उदाहरण:

  • सड़क पर यातायात संकेत जैसे लाल बत्ती, हरी बत्ती, पीली बत्ती।
  • खेल के निशान जैसे गोल, तीर, या तख्ता।
  • कंप्यूटर आइकॉन जैसे डेस्कटॉप पर ट्रैश या फाइल का चिन्ह।

3. महत्व: ये संकेत हमें बिना बोले, तुरंत जानकारी देने में मदद करते हैं। जैसे सड़क संकेत हमें सड़क पर चलने का सही तरीका बताते हैं।

4. प्रयोग: हम अपने रोज़ के जीवन में इन संकेतों का प्रयोग करते हैं। उदाहरण के लिए, घर में "ध्यान दें" का चिन्ह, या स्कूल में "सावधान" का संकेत।

5. सामान्य संकेत:

  • यातायात संकेत
  • खेल के निशान
  • कंप्यूटर और मोबाइल के आइकॉन
  • सार्वजनिक स्थानों पर संकेत जैसे "शौचालय", "प्रवेश निषेध"

Worked Example

मान लीजिए कि आपको सड़क पर लाल बत्ती का चिन्ह दिखता है। इसका मतलब है कि गाड़ी रुक जाए। यह सांकेतिक न है, क्योंकि यह शब्दों के बिना ही हमें बता रहा है कि हमें रुकना है। इसी तरह, हरी बत्ती का चिन्ह दिखने पर हमें आगे बढ़ना चाहिए। यह संकेत हमें सुरक्षित और सही तरीके से चलने में मदद करता है।

Common Mistake

अक्सर छात्र सोचते हैं कि सांकेतिक न केवल चित्र या चिन्ह होते हैं, बल्कि शब्द भी हो सकते हैं। हकीकत में, सांकेतिक न वे चिन्ह या संकेत हैं जो बिना शब्दों के बात समझाते हैं। शब्द भी संकेत हो सकते हैं, लेकिन सामान्यतः सांकेतिक न चित्र, चिन्ह या संकेत होते हैं।

Quick Check Question

सड़क पर लाल बत्ती का चिन्ह किस बात का संकेत देता है?

  • (क) चलो
  • (ख) रुक जाओ
  • (ग) तेज चलो
  • (घ) चलने का समय

Summary

  • सांकेतिक न वे चिन्ह या संकेत हैं जो बिना शब्दों के बात समझाते हैं।
  • ये संकेत हमारे जीवन में बहुत उपयोगी हैं, जैसे यातायात संकेत, खेल के निशान, और कंप्यूटर आइकॉन।
  • इन संकेतों का सही प्रयोग हमें सुरक्षित और आसान जीवन जीने में मदद करता है।

Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

  • सुरक्षित रहने में मदद करते हैं।
  • दूरी पर भी संवाद संभव बनाते हैं।
  • बिना अनुमान के समझ आने में सहायता करते हैं।
  • परिस्थितियों के अनुसार संदेश बदलते हैं।
  • भावनाओं को बिना शब्दों के व्यक्त किया जाता है।

🧪 Worked ExamplesExamples

Lesson on Worked Examples for साङके न (Sanket)

What you will learn

In this lesson, you will learn how to solve a typical problem related to Sanket (signs or signals) using step-by-step reasoning, especially focusing on understanding how to interpret data and apply logical steps to reach the correct answer.

Simple explanation

A Sanket (sign) often gives us important information or clues about a situation. When solving problems, it’s essential to understand what the sign indicates and how to connect it with the data provided. We will see how to analyze the given data carefully and use logical steps to find the solution.

Step-by-step breakdown

1. Read the problem carefully: Understand what the problem is asking for.

2. Identify the data given: Note down all the numbers, signs, or clues provided.

3. Understand the sign or symbol: Think about what the sign indicates in the context.

4. Plan your approach: Decide which mathematical or logical method to use.

5. Perform calculations or reasoning: Follow the steps logically to arrive at the answer.

6. Check your answer: Make sure your solution makes sense in the context of the problem.

Worked Example

Question:

The following data shows the number of different items prepared for a festival:

  • Flowers: 20
  • Fruits: 15
  • Sweets: 25
  • Toys: 10

If the sign (Sanket) indicates that the item with the highest number is the main item for the festival, which item is it?

Step 1:

Identify the data: Flowers (20), Fruits (15), Sweets (25), Toys (10).

Step 2:

Look at the numbers: 20, 15, 25, 10.

Step 3:

Find the highest number: 25.

Step 4:

Match the highest number with the item: Sweets have 25.

Step 5:

Conclusion: Since sweets have the highest number, sweets are the main item for the festival.

Conceptual interpretation:

The sign (Sanket) here tells us that the item with the most quantity is the main focus. So, sweets are the main item for the festival.

Common mistake

Students sometimes forget to check all the data carefully or assume the first highest number they see without comparing all items. Always verify all data points before concluding.

Quick check question

Suppose the data shows:

  • Balloons: 30
  • Candles: 20
  • Decorations: 25
  • Snacks: 15

Which item is the main item if the sign indicates the item with the highest number?

---

Summary

In this lesson, you learned how to interpret data with signs and signs (Sanket) to identify the main item or important clue. Always carefully read the data, compare the numbers, and use logical reasoning to find the correct answer. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

📌 RecapSummary

Lesson on Recap of साङके न (Festivals and Celebrations)

1. What you will learn

  • We will review what festivals and celebrations are.
  • We will understand why festivals are important.
  • We will learn about some common festivals and how people celebrate them.
  • We will see how festivals bring people together and create happiness.

2. Simple explanation

Festivals are special days that people celebrate to remember important events, gods, or seasons. These days are filled with joy, music, dance, and special foods. Festivals help us stay connected with our traditions and make us happy. Different festivals are celebrated in different parts of our country, and each has its own special way of celebration.

3. Step-by-step breakdown

  • What are festivals?

Festivals are days when people celebrate something special, like a religious event or a change in seasons.

  • Why do we celebrate festivals?

Festivals remind us of our culture, history, and beliefs. They bring happiness and help us stay connected with family and friends.

  • Examples of festivals:
  • Holi: Celebrated with colors and water, symbolizing joy and new beginnings.
  • Diwali: Festival of lights, where lamps and candles are lit to show victory of good over evil.
  • Raksha Bandhan: Celebrating the bond between brothers and sisters.
  • How are festivals celebrated?

People wear new clothes, prepare special foods, decorate their homes, and participate in dances and prayers.

  • Why are festivals important?

They promote unity, happiness, and help us remember our traditions.

4. Worked example

Suppose we are talking about Holi.

  • People gather in open spaces.
  • They throw colored powders and water on each other.
  • They sing and dance.
  • People visit friends and family to exchange greetings.
  • Special sweets like gujiya are prepared and shared.

This helps everyone feel happy and connected.

5. Common mistake

Many students think festivals are only about fun and games.

Correction: Festivals are also about remembering our traditions, praying, and spending time with loved ones. The fun is a way to celebrate the true meaning of the festival.

6. Quick check question

Name two festivals celebrated in India and describe one way people celebrate each.

7. Summary

Festivals are special days filled with happiness, prayers, and celebrations. They help us remember our traditions and bring us closer to our family and friends. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

Q: साङके न शब्द का मूल अर्थ क्या है?

  • शब्द इस शब्दका मूल है कि किसी चीज को सूचित करने के लिए हाथ या वस्तु का प्रयोग।
  • यह शब्द मुख्य रूप से प्रयोग किया जाता है जब किसी चीज को आँखों से दिखाना हो।
  • यह हिन्दी में ध्वनि पर आधारित है और सभी बोलियों में समान रूप से प्रचलित है।

For Sankranti, people decorate their homes in many beautiful ways. They often use colorful decorations like flowers, lights, and rangoli (patterns made on the ground with colored powders). Sometimes, they hang buntings and lanterns to make the house look bright and festive. These decorations help everyone feel happy and excited to celebrate the special festival.

Q: What are some common activities during Sankranti?

During Sankranti, some common activities include flying colorful kites, taking a bath in holy rivers, preparing special sweets like tilgul, and offering prayers to the Sun God. People also visit temples, celebrate with family and friends, and enjoy singing and dancing. These activities help everyone celebrate the festival happily and show gratitude to nature and the Sun.

Q: Why do people visit temples during Sankranti?

People visit temples during Sankranti to pray and seek blessings. It is a special festival, and visiting temples helps them show respect to God and ask for good health, happiness, and prosperity. Many people also believe that doing prayers during this time brings good luck and positive energy into their lives.

Q: What are some traditional foods prepared during Sankranti?

During Sankranti, people prepare special traditional foods like tilgul (sesame and jaggery sweets), ladoos, and chikkis (peanut and jaggery bars). Q: How are Sankranti celebrations different from other festivals?

Sankranti celebrations are different from other festivals because they mainly celebrate the change of seasons, especially the transition of the Sun into a new zodiac sign. People fly kites and enjoy special foods like sweets made from sesame and jaggery. Unlike some festivals that focus on religious rituals or specific gods, Sankranti is more about thanking nature and enjoying the outdoors with family and friends. It is a harvest festival that marks the end of winter and the start of longer days.

📖 5. सुंदरिया

🎯 What We LearnIntroduction

What We Learn in Chapter 5: सुंदरिया

1. What you will learn

In this lesson, we will understand how measurements are made using traditional Indian units and how they relate to modern units. We will explore the story of सुंदरिया to see how different objects are measured and compare these measurements. We will also learn about different units like कोल, क्वांटा, नाव, and how they are used to measure things like milk, length, and weight.

2. Simple explanation

Measurement is about finding out how big, long, or heavy something is. In the past, people used units like कोल, क्वांटा, and नाव to measure things. These units are different from the units we use today, like meters and kilograms. Understanding these old units helps us see how measurements were done earlier and how they connect to modern measurements.

3. Step-by-step breakdown

Step 1: Understanding traditional units

  • कोल: A small measure used for liquids like milk.
  • क्वांटा: A unit used for measuring weight.
  • नाव: A unit used for measuring length, especially for boats or distances.

Step 2: Relating old units to modern units

  • These units were used in ancient times and are still sometimes used in rural areas.
  • For example, a कोल might be about the amount of milk a small container holds.
  • A नाव could be a certain length used for measuring distances or boats.

Step 3: Story of सुंदरिया and measurement

  • सुंदरिया’s story shows how people measured milk, animals, and distances.
  • The story mentions that a cow’s milk was measured in "पाँव" (pao), which is a traditional unit.
  • It also shows how measurements like "दो सौ रुपए" (200 rupees) were used to buy animals, which relates to weight and value.

Step 4: Why measurement is important

  • It helps us understand the size, weight, and quantity of things.
  • Knowing old units helps us understand stories and traditions better.

Step 5: Activity of measurement

  • Find out how much milk a container holds in your home.
  • Compare it with the traditional unit कोल.
  • Think about how these measurements help in daily life.

Step 6: Connecting measurements to real life

  • For example, a boat’s length (नाव) helps in navigation.
  • The weight of animals (क्वांटा) helps in selling and buying.
  • Milk measurement (कोल) helps in cooking and selling.

4. Worked example

Suppose a cow gives 10 कोल of milk daily. If 1 कोल equals 1 liter, then:

  • Total milk per day = 10 कोल × 1 liter = 10 liters.
  • If you want to buy 5 कोल of milk, how much milk will you get?
  • Calculation: 5 कोल × 1 liter = 5 liters.
  • So, you will get 5 liters of milk.

This shows how traditional units relate to modern liters, making measurement easier to understand and use.

5. Common mistake

Many students think that old units like कोल and नाव are not useful anymore. But these units are important because they help us understand history, culture, and how measurement evolved over time. Always remember their connection to modern units.

6. Quick check question

If a container holds 3 कोल of milk and 1 कोल equals 1 liter, how much milk is in the container?

7. Summary

  • Measurement helps us find out the size, weight, and quantity of things.
  • Traditional units like कोल, क्वांटा, and नाव were used in the past.
  • These units are connected to modern units like liters and kilograms.
  • Understanding measurement helps us in daily life and in appreciating history.
  • Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.

🧪 Worked ExamplesExamples

Lesson on Meters and Measurement Units in the Context of "सुंदरिया"

1. What you will learn

Today, we will learn about different units of measurement, especially focusing on how length, weight, and volume are measured using traditional units like "से ि" (se) and other units. We will also understand why these units are important and how they are used in real life, especially in the context of the story "सुंदरिया."

2. Simple explanation

In our story, characters talk about measuring things like milk, animals, and distances. To do this accurately, they use units like "से ि" (se), "रिा्चीन" (measurement units), and others. These units help us understand how big, heavy, or long something is. Just like we use centimeters or kilograms today, earlier people used units like "से ि" to measure.

3. Step-by-step breakdown

Step 1: Understanding Measurement Units

  • Units are ways to tell how big or small something is.
  • In the story, "से ि" (se) was used to measure milk quantity.
  • Other units like "रिा्चीन" (measurement units) were also used for measuring length, weight, and volume.

Step 2: Why are units important?

  • Units help us compare different things.
  • For example, knowing that one cow gives more milk than another helps decide which cow to buy.
  • Without units, measurements are meaningless.

Step 3: Traditional units in the story

  • "से ि" (se): Used to measure milk quantity.
  • "रिा्चीन": Used for measuring length or height.
  • "क्ुआ" (kuaa): A unit used for measuring volume or capacity.
  • "नाव" (nau): Used for measuring distance or length.

Step 4: Practical examples from the story

  • The farmer talks about a cow that gives "दो सौ रुपए तक लग ्च ुक े हैं" (up to 200 rupees worth of milk).
  • The merchant mentions "पाँ ्च हम जयादा दे देंगे" (we will give five more).
  • These measurements help in deciding the price and value.

Step 5: How to measure in real life

  • Use a measuring tape for length (like "रिा्चीन").
  • Use a weighing scale for weight.
  • Use a container or measuring jug for volume (like "क्ुआ").
  • These tools help us measure accurately.

Step 6: Activity

  • Think about how you measure your height or the milk you drink.
  • Which units do you use? (Centimeters, liters, etc.)
  • Discuss with your family about units they use in daily life.

4. Worked example

Suppose you want to buy milk and the shopkeeper says, "This milk is 1 से" (1 se). If 1 se equals 1 liter, then:

  • The milk quantity is 1 liter.
  • If you want more milk, you ask for 2 से, which means 2 liters.
  • This way, units help you know exactly how much milk you are buying.

5. Common mistake

Many students think that units are just labels and don't affect the measurement. Remember, units are essential because they tell us how much of something we have. Without units, measurements are meaningless.

6. Quick check question

If a cow gives 3 से of milk each day, and 1 से equals 1 liter, how much milk does the cow give in 3 days?

7. Summary

Today, we learned about measurement units like "से ि," "रिा्चीन," "क्ुआ," and "नाव." These units help us measure length, weight, and volume accurately. They are very important in daily life, especially in buying and selling things like milk and animals. Review these key points, then move to the next lesson step when ready.
